Um... there hasn't really been a week where I haven't had at least one of those... moments where I eat too much in private. And then... you know, I try to fix it. By making myself sick, or just... not eating the next day, or walking for hours. It's... it's at least once a week, usually.\n\nDoctor: How often do you eat in private and then feel like you've had too much?\n\nPatient: Well... like I said, about once a week. It usually happens when... when no one else is in the kitchen. \n\nI just... I end up going through whatever is around. Leftovers from a church supper, crackers, cheese... cookies, or whatever I baked for my book club. I eat it all in one sitting. It's... well, it's far more than I would ever actually serve myself at a meal.\n\nDoctor: How many times a week do you eat in private and then feel like you've had too much?\n\nPatient: Um... like I just mentioned, it's... about once a week. Usually.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat in private and feel like you've had too much more than once a week?\n\nPatient: I... well, no, not usually. Like I've been saying... it's just that since last winter, there has hardly been a single week where I haven't had at least one of those... spells. And then had to try and make up for it by throwing up, or skipping my meals, or... overdoing my walking. But... it's generally just the once. I don't really keep a strict count.\n\nDoctor: Do you have episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short time frame more than once a week?\n\nPatient: I... I think I just answered that. It's... it's just the once a week, usually. Like I said, since last winter, it's just been... about one time a week where I have one of those spells and then have to... fix it, by being sick or fasting or walking too much. Just... just once a week.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself sick, or fast, or exercise excessively when you haven't eaten a large amount of food in a short time frame?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. It's really just... when I have one of those spells. That's the only time I feel like I need to... do something about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use laxatives or diuretics to try and make up for eating too much?\n\nPatient: No... I've never done that. I just... stick to what I mentioned before.\n\nDoctor: Do you make yourself sick, fast, or exercise excessively to prevent weight gain after eating too much?\n\nPatient: Yes... I mean, that's actually why I finally decided to come in. After I have one of those spells where I eat too much... I'll go upstairs and make myself throw up. I just... I try to act like I'm just tidying up the kitchen or getting ready for bed, so nobody notices. But yes, I do that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short time frame without feeling a loss of control?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. It's... it's always the same. When I eat like that, I just... I can't stop it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past year?\n\nPatient: No... not really. It's stayed about the same, I think.\n\nDoctor: Do you make yourself sick after eating too much specifically to undo the calories you've consumed?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure... I don't think about it like that, with the calories. I just... I just feel like I need to get rid of it. That's all.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

{"intent": "determine if the patient's behavior is aimed at preventing weight gain", "question": "Is making yourself sick after eating too much related to trying to maintain your current weight?"}
